Uniform all-edge scaling can't do this (gives constant ratio). => fault structure is config-dependent => measure ΔM directly: for each block b, set b at max, others at min, read_all; subtract m2 (all-min baseline, already have); compare to model rc_run same config. 9 sensors per config → ΔM column. 308NoteANALYSIS with verified factory baselines (r20=B,r21=A,r22=C,r23=D,r24=E,r25=F,r26=G): real rise/model rise ratios are ~1.15-1.20 UNIFORMLY at configs A,D,E,F,G,B (e.g. B core0 49/41.05=1.19, pkg 33/27.6=1.20; G avg 1.15; E fits x1.15 to ~1C). But config C (all cores 150%, 92.7W) needs factor ~1.5: extra ~+13..22C beyond x1.15, larger on hotter cores (core spread real 24C vs model 13C). So: (1) a global linear fault ~+15% on all rises, PLUS (2) a distinct extra effect only at config C. Superposition failure (G != (A+C)/2) confirmed with trusted data. Extra at C correlates with temperature (not uniform shift, not ambient). Leading hypotheses: global boundary-conductance fault (model G too high ~15%) + temperature-threshold degradation above ~95-105C junction; OR global power_gain +15% (but then tau unchanged - m4 suggested real tau 0.45-0.5 vs model ~0.38, checking now) + something at C. PLAN: (a) verify model tau; (b) test cores@125% (82W total) to locate threshold; (c) test cores@nominal + noncores@max (90W, per-core 100%) to separate per-core-power vs temp/total-power threshold; (d) test 4 cores@150% only (52W, hot cores cooler) to test own-temp vs global.
309Propose h4Two-fault structure: (1) a global LINEAR fault making all temperature rises ~15-20% higher than the factory model at every low/moderate-power config (A,D,E,F,G,B all fit rise x ~1.15-1.20 uniformly across all 9 sensors); mechanism either global power delivery gain (+15%) or global thermal conductance deficit (model conductance ~15-17% too high). (2) An ADDITIONAL effect present only at config C (all cores at 150% nominal, 92.7W total): extra +13..22C beyond the x1.15 prediction, growing with temperature (core spread doubles), ruling out ambient shift or uniform gain. Candidates: temperature-threshold degradation (e.g. boundary conductance drops above ~95-105C junction), per-block-power threshold above ~1.1x nominal, or total-power threshold between 80 and 92.7W.
